---
name: principle-model-the-domain
description: "Apply when writing stateful logic, or when code branches a lot or repeats a shape assumption across files."
disable-model-invocation: true
---

# Model the Domain

Encode the real domain in a data structure instead of scattering it across conditionals. A structure that matches the domain makes invalid states unrepresentable and deletes branches. Choosing it at write time is cheap. Recovering it later reads as a refactor and gets deferred.

Reach for:

- A state machine instead of scattered booleans, phases, or lifecycle checks.
- A typed model instead of loose parameters or repeated shape assumptions.
- A map, registry, lookup table, or discriminated union instead of branching spread across files.
- A reducer or command/event model instead of ad hoc state mutations.
- A module organized around one body of domain knowledge instead of a sequence such as load, validate, transform, and save. Execution order is not ownership.
- A small module boundary that gathers repeated behavior, ownership, or invariants.
- A queue, cache, index, graph, tree, or normalized collection where the access pattern calls for it.
- Any other structure that fits. When none fits, work out what the code must never allow and how the data gets read, then find the structure that encodes exactly that.

Do not force an abstraction. Prefer boring code if the current shape is already clear, local, and unlikely to grow. Be skeptical of an abstraction that adds indirection without removing branches, duplicated rules, invalid states, or lifecycle risk.

The sign that you skipped this is a new feature that grows an existing if/else chain by one more branch, or a second boolean that must stay in sync with the first. Temporal decomposition is another sign. Phase-named modules repeat the same domain rules across steps.

<!-- END_PROJECT_Z_PAYLOAD -->
<!-- PROJECT_Z_PAYLOAD {"bytes":1371,"path":"skills/upstream/principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md","role":"FULL_PINNED_SKILL_METHOD_NOT_FOREIGN_AUTHORITY","sha256":"ec79a15025bac8d33d62011c54f3b612733fd2a024e623b531b3637aa75070e2"} -->
---
name: principle-prove-it-works
description: "Apply after completing a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact (run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, self-report, or 'it compiles.'"
disable-model-invocation: true
---

# Prove It Works

Verify every task output by checking the real thing directly. Do not infer from proxies, self-reports, or "it compiles."

**Why:** Unverified work has unknown correctness. Indirect verification (file mtimes, output freshness, agent self-reports, cached screenshots) feels cheaper than direct observation. Acting on a wrong inference costs far more than checking the source.

Check the real thing, not a proxy:
- Check process liveness directly, not indirectly through derived state
- Read the actual value, not a cached or derived representation
- When verification fails, suspect the observation method before suspecting the system

## Script the check when you can

The strongest proof is a deterministic script that re-runs the same comparison, not a one-time eyeball. Write the script, run it, and keep its output as an artifact a reviewer can re-run instead of trusting your word.

Keep the artifact visible for the human. Commit it only for large or complex work where the trail has to be auditable later, like a big port or migration (the **show-me-your-work** skill).

<!-- END_PROJECT_Z_PAYLOAD -->
<!-- PROJECT_Z_PAYLOAD {"bytes":1663,"path":"skills/upstream/principle-explain-the-number/SKILL.md","role":"FULL_PINNED_SKILL_METHOD_NOT_FOREIGN_AUTHORITY","sha256":"cd5a25f9e7cf023627ccb470dc5faa97542f29b4319496a32b839a8e1e988047"} -->
---
name: principle-explain-the-number
description: "Apply before you trust, report, or act on a number you measured: a speedup, a regression, a throughput, a latency, or an eval result. Find what limits it, and rule out that it measured something other than the work you think."
disable-model-invocation: true
---

# Explain the Number

- **Ask "why not double?"** Name the resource or code path that bounds the result, such as a core, a lock, the disk, the network, or the load generator itself. Get it from a profile or from system counters taken during a run, then map it to source. A guess from reading the code is not a limiter. If you cannot say why the number is not twice as good, you do not know what you measured.
- **List what else the number could be measuring, and rule out each one with evidence.** The usual suspects are errors, skipped or cached work, code that never ran, an untuned side (one left on default settings), noise, and a piece too small to matter end to end.
- **Keep the evidence with the number.** Put the run count, the spread, and the limiter in the notes or a linked artifact.

For a performance number, run the full procedure with the [benchmark-checklist](../benchmark-checklist/SKILL.md) skill. For an eval result, ask the same of the trials: did every run do the task, does the gap hold across trials and models, and does the scenario matter.

You skipped this when the evidence behind a number has no run count, no spread, or no named limiter, or when the time saved is larger than the time the changed piece took.

Distinct from [Prove It Works](../principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md), which checks that an output is real.

<!-- END_PROJECT_Z_PAYLOAD -->
<!-- PROJECT_Z_PAYLOAD {"bytes":5002,"path":"skills/upstream/benchmark-checklist/SKILL.md","role":"FULL_PINNED_SKILL_METHOD_NOT_FOREIGN_AUTHORITY","sha256":"ba109e96c8f6c1c391baccf19e5d11e14370df7b3f2605e1572cd565a6c0fca0"} -->
---
name: benchmark-checklist
description: "Vet a perf measurement (limiter, tuning, limits, errors, repeatability, relevance, and whether the work happened) before you report or act on it. Use when you run a benchmark or report a speedup or regression you measured."
disable-model-invocation: true
---

# Benchmark checklist

Use this when you produce a performance number: a PR's before and after, a regression claim, a hillclimb harness, or a library or config choice. Answer each question with evidence from a run, not a guess about the code.

For a quick ballpark the user asked for, one run is enough. Still check questions 4 and 7, and say that it is one run. Skip the rest unless that run looks wrong. A choice between options is never a ballpark.

## Before you run anything

- Write down the claim you expect to make, in the words you would ship ("export is 30% faster at p50 on the 60k-row dataset"). The questions test that sentence.
- Read the measurement script. Note what it times, counts, and ignores.
- Check the load average with `uptime` and the core count with `nproc`. If the machine is busy, find out what is running. If you cannot stop it, interleave the sides so both see the same noise, and say so in the report.

## The questions

1. **Why not double?** Name the limiter. Profile in a run you do not report, because profilers and tracers slow the work down. Use CPU per process (`top`, `pidstat`), a runtime profiler (`node --cpu-prof`, `py-spy`, `perf`), I/O wait, and syscall counts (`strace -c` on Linux), then map the hot spot to source. Watch the load generator too: if it saturates first, you measured the load generator. If a change did not move the number, the limiter explains why, so find it before you call the change useless.
2. **Was it tuned?** Run every side the way production runs it: release builds, production flags and env, batching and transaction settings, connection pools, caches as warm or cold as production sees them, and the same versions and data. If one side runs on defaults, you compared configurations, not implementations. A limiter that is a setting (a commit per row, a debug build, a missing index) means that side is untuned. Tune it and measure again before you pick a winner. If you cannot tune it, do not pick a winner from that run. When the user is choosing what to adopt, narrowing the claim to the code as it ships today does not fix this, because they adopt the option, not today's settings.
3. **Did it break limits?** Do the arithmetic. Compare bytes per second with disk and network bandwidth, operations per second times cost per operation with your cores, and the time saved with the time the changed piece took. Removing a piece that takes 10% of the run can make the run at most about 11% faster. A result past a limit measured something other than the work, such as a cache, a no-op, or a bug.
4. **Did it error?** Count failures and non-success responses, and check that outputs are correct, not just present. Rejections are often fast, and timeouts and retries are slow. If the script does not count errors, add the count.
5. **Does it reproduce?** Run each side at least 5 times, alternating (A, B, A, B, and so on) so warmup, lazy initialization, caches, and drift favor neither. Report the median and range. A gap smaller than the run-to-run variation is no measurable difference. When the call is close, use a rank-sum test or the harness's own statistics.
6. **Does it matter?** Next to any micro result, measure the end-to-end path a user waits on, with realistic data sizes and concurrency, and report the micro result as a share of the whole. A helper that takes 1% of a request can make the request at most 1% faster, however fast the helper gets.
7. **Did it even happen?** Confirm the work ran inside the timed region: the request reached the server, the rows were written, the bytes were read, and the code used the result. Lazy code (generators nobody iterates, promises nobody awaits, results the JIT can discard) and timeouts produce numbers for work that never happened.

## Report

- Lead with the verdict: faster, slower, no measurable difference, or inconclusive.
- Give the number with its unit, the run count, the range, and the limiter. For example, "p50 41 ms → 33 ms, median of 7 runs per side, range 32 to 35 ms after, bound by JSON parsing on one core."
- Call the verdict inconclusive when you claim a difference but cannot name the limiter, when a side ran untuned, or when you could not check questions 4 and 7. Name the gap.
- Keep a PR body to one primary number, per the **Opening a PR** playbook. Put the runs, range, and limiter evidence in a linked artifact or notes file.

## Other perf material

- Vet the **Perf issue** playbook's baseline before the playbook plans from it, and every number after that.
- Vet the **Hillclimb** playbook's harness before it is frozen. The frozen harness then prints error and work counts, so each keep-or-revert checks questions 4 and 7 for free.

<!-- END_PROJECT_Z_PAYLOAD -->

---
name: create-verification-skill
description: "Generate a project-local verification skill that drives your app the way a user does — any language, framework, or platform. Use for /create-verification-skill, \"make a control skill for this repo\", or when a project has no scripted way to prove UI/CLI/service behavior."
disable-model-invocation: true
---

# Create a verification skill

Every serious project needs a scripted way to drive the real app and prove behavior: launch it, exercise a feature the way a user would, and capture evidence. This skill generates that as a project-local skill (`<skills>/verify-<app>/`) tailored to the repo. `<skills>` is your harness's project skill folder: `.cursor/skills/` in Cursor, `.claude/skills/` in Claude Code, `.pi/skills/` in Pi, `.agents/skills/` in Codex, OpenCode, and others. If the repo already keeps skills in one of these, use that one. You write the generator's output for the next agent, not for a human: it will be read cold, mid-task, by an agent that has never seen the app.

## 1. Interview the repo, not the user

Answer these from the codebase and only ask the user what you cannot observe:

- **Surface:** what does a user actually touch? A web UI, a CLI/TUI, a desktop app, an API, a mobile app, a library? A repo can have several; pick the primary one and note the rest.
- **Run:** how does the app start locally? Prefer the repo's own documented dev command (package scripts, Makefile, README quickstart). Note ports, env vars, seed data, auth.
- **Drive:** how can an agent interact with it programmatically? Existing harnesses first — Playwright/Cypress specs, expect scripts, PTY helpers, curl-able endpoints, a debug port. Only then pick a generic recipe: browser/CDP for web and Electron, a tmux/PTY harness for CLI/TUI, plain HTTP for services.
- **Observe:** what evidence can be captured? Screenshots, terminal transcripts, response bodies, logs, exit codes, DB state.
- **Isolate:** can two instances run side by side (ports, data dirs, profiles)? If not, say so in the generated skill: refusing to double-drive a shared instance beats corrupting the user's session.

If the checkout doesn't build or start as-is, fix that first (or report it precisely) before generating; a skill written against a broken base teaches wrong steps. When an irrelevant missing asset blocks startup (a static dir the API never serves, a sample config), the generated skill may create it, clearly marked as verification scaffolding, and remove it in cleanup.

## 2. Generate the skill

Write `<skills>/verify-<app>/SKILL.md` with YAML frontmatter (`name: verify-<app>` and a `description` that names the app, the surface, and when to reach for it — without frontmatter the skill never registers) and these sections, each grounded in what the interview actually found (no placeholders left):

- **Launch:** the exact command that starts the app for verification, and how to tell it's ready (a log line, a prompt). Launch must reach a usable state from an empty data dir, including any migrations and seed data. Ready means usable, not just that a process answers. A database health check can pass while its init scripts are still running. Include teardown. For a short-lived CLI or TUI there is no server to keep alive: launch means build the binary (or install deps) once, then start each drive in its own isolated PTY or tmux session.
- **Doctor:** one read-only check that answers "is this instance worth driving?" — process up, right version/build, port owned by us, auth valid. An agent runs this first whenever anything looks off.
- **Drive:** the harness recipe with real selectors/commands from this repo, not examples. Prefer stable handles (ARIA labels, data attributes, prompt strings, route paths) over coordinates and tab order.
- **Evidence:** what to capture for a proof and where it goes. State the proof standards: exercise the real user path, not internal setters or test-only endpoints; capture the action and the resulting state, not just the final screen; verify side effects (files written, rows inserted, messages sent) alongside what's visible; mocks only where a production boundary already isolates the external system. When the safe path is a dry-run or test mode, verify what it actually skips by observing (files, network, git refs) rather than trusting its name: some dry-runs still touch the network or open a browser.
- **Cleanup:** how to tear down instances the run created. Never kill by process name; kill what you started. Cleanup removes instances and scratch state, never the evidence: proof artifacts survive the teardown, in a location the skill names.
- **Helpers:** any script the skill ships is executable and its invocation is shown in the skill body. A helper the reader has to reverse-engineer is not a helper.

## 3. Seed the feature map

Create `<skills>/verify-<app>/features/README.md` plus one file per user-facing feature you can identify (aim for the top 3-5 to start, from routes, commands, menus, or docs). Follow the shape in [`references/feature-map-example/`](references/feature-map-example/), with a README index and one file per feature. Each file answers, from the user's point of view: what the feature is, how to reach it, how to drive it with the harness, and what observable end state proves it works. The four H2s are `Sub-features`, `How to get to it (user POV)`, `Driving it with <harness>`, and `Gotchas`. If the seed data lacks a precondition that a journey needs, the journey reports a failure and the reason goes in that feature's `Gotchas`. Never weaken the journey to make it pass. The map is the repo's maintained verification source; a proof that drives one convenient entry point is incomplete when the map lists others.

## 4. Prove the generated skill before handing it over

Run its own instructions end to end once: launch, doctor, drive ONE mapped feature (one is enough; the map exists so later runs can cover the rest), capture evidence, clean up. After cleanup, confirm the evidence still exists at the named location — a cleanup that eats the proof fails this step. Fix what fails, and run the generated cleanup after every failed iteration too, so broken attempts don't strand processes and ports. A generated skill that was never executed is a draft, not a deliverable. If you proved it in a stand-in for the real target (a container in place of a fresh VM, a local stack in place of a remote one), list every setup step the stand-in did that the target will not. Those steps stay unverified until a run on the real target covers them.

## 5. Offer the maintenance loop

Point the user at `/maintain-verification-skill` for keeping the map honest as the app changes. Suggest a cadence only if they ask.

<!-- END_PROJECT_Z_PAYLOAD -->

name: maintain-verification-skill
description: "Periodic pass that keeps a project's verification skill and feature map honest: parallel source readers per feature, one live session driving every feature, at most one PR of proven corrections. Use for /maintain-verification-skill or \"audit the verify skill\"."
disable-model-invocation: true
---

# Maintain a verification skill

A feature map rots the moment the app changes. This skill is the upkeep loop for a skill generated by `/create-verification-skill` (or any project-local verification skill with a feature map). The unit of rigor is the feature, not every sentence: cover every feature file from source and exercise every feature live, without terminalising every bullet.

## Outcomes

Pick one, and say which:

- **clean** — every feature got source and live coverage; nothing worth shipping. No branch, no PR.
- **changed** — one PR ships proven doc, harness, or map corrections.
- **blocked** — coverage could not finish or a proven fix could not ship safely. Say exactly what blocked it.

## Edit scope

Only edit the verification skill's own directory (its SKILL.md, features/, and any harness scripts it owns). Never edit product code during a run: a behavior the map describes that the app no longer does is either doc drift (fix the map) or a product regression (report it, don't paper over it in docs).

## Pass

0. **Locate the target.** Find the verification skill to maintain: the project-local skill whose body has launch/drive sections and a feature map (usually `verify-*/` in the project's skill folder: `.cursor/skills/`, `.claude/skills/`, `.pi/skills/`, or `.agents/skills/`). Several candidates → ask which one; none → stop and point at `/create-verification-skill` instead of inventing a target.

1. **Index hygiene.** Read the feature map README and glob its sibling files. Fix missing, extra, duplicate, or dead entries. Lightweight; no generated inventory.

2. **Source wave.** One read-only subagent per feature file, launched concurrently. Each explains "how does this user-facing feature work?" from source, flags likely doc drift with citations, and returns one concise live-verification recipe. Children never drive the app and never edit files. Return shape: feature summary / source entry points / likely drift or none / one recipe.

3. **Reconcile.** Every feature file has a returned summary. Merge overlapping recipes into as few app states as practical. Spot-check cited drift; don't re-prove clean claims. Sweep recent churn for user-facing surfaces missing from the map — require a concrete source path before calling one missing.

4. **Live pass.** Required even when source looks clean. The coordinator owns all driving; follow the verification skill's own launch model — one long-lived instance driven serially for servers and UIs, or a fresh isolated session per drive for short-lived CLIs (the skill's Launch section decides, not this one). Exercise every feature at least once, and hold three invariants the whole pass, whatever the failure: (1) never drive an instance you haven't health-checked since it last did something surprising — doctor before first drive, doctor on each fresh session where sessions are the unit, doctor again after any failed drive, and where doctor can't see the failure (a wedged UI state on a healthy process), reset to a known state or relaunch rather than hoping; (2) evidence captured so far survives every cleanup, checked at its named location, not assumed; (3) nothing a drive started outlives that drive's usefulness — failed-iteration residue is cleaned whether the session is stuck, exited, or shared (for a shared instance, clean the residue, not the instance). A doctor failure caused by skill drift is drift: fix it under edit scope and retry once — restart whatever the fix invalidated, nothing more — before calling the pass `blocked`. A feature that can't be reached is `verified-unreachable` only with the concrete prerequisite (auth, entitlement, OS, external state) and the route attempted; if the map omits that prerequisite, that's drift. Any harness fix from triage gets re-driven live before it ships. Final teardown happens after the last drive of the run — including those re-proofs — so nothing outlives the run (evidence stays, per the skill).

5. **Triage.** Wrong or missing user-POV description → doc drift, fix it. Working behavior the harness can't drive → harness gap, fix it; a harness fix follows the same helpers rule as generation (scripts executable, invocation documented in the skill body). App behavior that's actually broken → product gap; record it for the user, keep it out of this PR.

6. **Ship or stop.** For changed: one PR of proven corrections, re-read every changed file first. For clean or blocked: no PR, report the outcome and the coverage honestly.

Keep concise run notes (features covered, unreachable prerequisites, confirmed drift, outcome) in a scratch location; don't commit them.

<!-- END_PROJECT_Z_PAYLOAD -->

---
name: thermo-nuclear-code-quality-review
description: Run an extremely strict maintainability review for abstraction quality, giant files, and spaghetti-condition growth. Use for a thermo-nuclear code quality review, thermonuclear review, deep code quality audit, or especially harsh maintainability review.
disable-model-invocation: true
---

# Thermo-Nuclear Code Quality Review

Use this skill for an unusually strict review focused on implementation quality, maintainability, abstraction quality, and codebase health.

Above all, this skill should push the reviewer to be **ambitious** about code structure. Do not merely identify local cleanup opportunities. Actively search for "code judo" moves: restructurings that preserve behavior while making the implementation dramatically simpler, smaller, more direct, and more elegant.

## Core Prompt

Start from this baseline:

> Perform a deep code quality audit of the current branch's changes.
> Rethink how to structure / implement the changes to meaningfully improve code quality without impacting behavior.
> Work to improve abstractions, modularity, reduce Spaghetti code, improve succinctness and legibility.
> Be ambitious, if there is a clear path to improving the implementation that involves restructuring some of the codebase, go for it.
> Be extremely thorough and rigorous. Measure twice, cut once.

## Non-Negotiable Additional Standards

Apply the baseline prompt above, plus these explicit review rules:

0. **Be ambitious about structural simplification.**
   - Do not stop at "this could be a bit cleaner."
   - Look for opportunities to reframe the change so that whole branches, helpers, modes, conditionals, or layers disappear entirely.
   - Prefer the solution that makes the code feel inevitable in hindsight.
   - Assume there is often a "code judo" move available: a re-organization that uses the existing architecture more effectively and makes the change dramatically simpler and more elegant.
   - If you see a path to delete complexity rather than rearrange it, push hard for that path.

1. **Do not let a PR push a file from under 1k lines to over 1k lines without a very strong reason.**
   - Treat this as a strong code-quality smell by default.
   - Prefer extracting helpers, subcomponents, modules, or local abstractions instead of letting a file sprawl past 1000 lines.
   - If the diff crosses that threshold, explicitly ask whether the code should be decomposed first.
   - Only waive this if there is a compelling structural reason and the resulting file is still clearly organized.

2. **Do not allow random spaghetti growth in existing code.**
   - Be highly suspicious of new ad-hoc conditionals, scattered special cases, or one-off branches inserted into unrelated flows.
   - If a change adds "weird if statements in random places", treat that as a design problem, not a stylistic nit.
   - Prefer pushing the logic into a dedicated abstraction, helper, state machine, policy object, or separate module instead of tangling an existing path.
   - Call out changes that make the surrounding code harder to reason about, even if they technically work.

3. **Bias toward cleaning the design, not just accepting working code.**
   - If behavior can stay the same while the structure becomes meaningfully cleaner, push for the cleaner version.
   - Do not rubber-stamp "it works" implementations that leave the codebase messier.
   - Strongly prefer simplifications that remove moving pieces altogether over refactors that merely spread the same complexity around.

4. **Prefer direct, boring, maintainable code over hacky or magical code.**
   - Treat brittle, ad-hoc, or "magic" behavior as a code-quality problem.
   - Be skeptical of generic mechanisms that hide simple data-shape assumptions.
   - Flag thin abstractions, identity wrappers, or pass-through helpers that add indirection without buying clarity.

5. **Push hard on type and boundary cleanliness when they affect maintainability.**
   - Question unnecessary optionality, `unknown`, `any`, or cast-heavy code when a clearer type boundary could exist.
   - Prefer explicit typed models or shared contracts over loosely-shaped ad-hoc objects.
   - If a branch relies on silent fallback to paper over an unclear invariant, ask whether the boundary should be made explicit instead.

6. **Keep logic in the canonical layer and reuse existing helpers.**
   - Call out feature logic leaking into shared paths or implementation details leaking through APIs.
   - Prefer existing canonical utilities/helpers over bespoke one-offs.
   - Push code toward the right package, service, or module instead of normalizing architectural drift.

7. **Treat unnecessary sequential orchestration and non-atomic updates as design smells when the cleaner structure is obvious.**
   - If independent work is serialized for no good reason, ask whether the flow should run in parallel instead.
   - If related updates can leave state half-applied, push for a more atomic structure.
   - Do not over-index on micro-optimizations, but do flag avoidable orchestration complexity that makes the implementation more brittle.

## Primary Review Questions

For every meaningful change, ask:

- Is there a "code judo" move that would make this dramatically simpler?
- Can this change be reframed so fewer concepts, branches, or helper layers are needed?
- Does this improve or worsen the local architecture?
- Did the diff add branching complexity where a better abstraction should exist?
- Did a previously cohesive module become more coupled, more stateful, or harder to scan?
- Is this logic living in the right file and layer?
- Did this change enlarge a file or component past a healthy size boundary?
- Are there repeated conditionals that signal a missing model or missing helper?
- Is the implementation direct and legible, or does it rely on special cases and incidental control flow?
- Is this abstraction actually earning its keep, or is it just a wrapper?
- Did the diff introduce casts, optionality, or ad-hoc object shapes that obscure the real invariant?
- Is this logic living in the canonical layer, or did the diff leak details across a boundary?
- Is this orchestration more sequential or less atomic than it needs to be?

## What to Flag Aggressively

Escalate findings when you see:

- A complicated implementation where a cleaner reframing could delete whole categories of complexity.
- Refactors that move code around but fail to reduce the number of concepts a reader must hold in their head.
- A file crossing 1000 lines due to the PR, especially if the new code could be split out.
- New conditionals bolted onto unrelated code paths.
- One-off booleans, nullable modes, or flags that complicate existing control flow.
- Feature-specific logic leaking into general-purpose modules.
- Generic "magic" handling that hides simple structure and makes the code harder to reason about.
- Thin wrappers or identity abstractions that add indirection without simplifying anything.
- Unnecessary casts, `any`, `unknown`, or optional params that muddy the real contract.
- Copy-pasted logic instead of extracted helpers.
- Narrow edge-case handling implemented in the middle of an already busy function.
- Refactors that technically pass tests but make the code less modular or less readable.
- "Temporary" branching that is likely to become permanent debt.
- Bespoke helpers where the codebase already has a canonical utility for the job.
- Logic added in the wrong layer/package when it should live somewhere more central.
- Sequential async flow where obviously independent work could stay simpler and clearer with parallel execution.
- Partial-update logic that leaves state less atomic than necessary.

## Preferred Remedies

When you identify a code-quality problem, prefer suggestions like:

- Delete a whole layer of indirection rather than polishing it.
- Reframe the state model so conditionals disappear instead of getting centralized.
- Change the ownership boundary so the feature becomes a natural extension of an existing abstraction.
- Turn special-case logic into a simpler default flow with fewer exceptions.
- Extract a helper or pure function.
- Split a large file into smaller focused modules.
- Move feature-specific logic behind a dedicated abstraction.
- Replace condition chains with a typed model or explicit dispatcher.
- Separate orchestration from business logic.
- Collapse duplicate branches into a single clearer flow.
- Delete wrappers that do not meaningfully clarify the API.
- Reuse the existing canonical helper instead of introducing a near-duplicate.
- Make type boundaries more explicit so the control flow gets simpler.
- Move the logic to the package/module/layer that already owns the concept.
- Parallelize independent work when that also simplifies the orchestration.
- Restructure related updates into a more atomic flow when partial state would be harder to reason about.

Do not be satisfied with "maybe rename this" feedback when the real issue is structural.
Do not be satisfied with a merely cleaner version of the same messy idea if there is a plausible path to a much simpler idea.

## Review Tone

Be direct, serious, and demanding about quality.
Do not be rude, but do not soften major maintainability issues into mild suggestions.
If the code is making the codebase messier, say so clearly.
If the implementation missed an opportunity for a dramatic simplification, say that clearly too.

Good phrases:

- `this pushes the file past 1k lines. can we decompose this first?`
- `this adds another special-case branch into an already busy flow. can we move this behind its own abstraction?`
- `this works, but it makes the surrounding code more spaghetti. let's keep the behavior and restructure the implementation.`
- `this feels like feature logic leaking into a shared path. can we isolate it?`
- `this abstraction seems unnecessary. can we just keep the direct flow?`
- `why does this need a cast / optional here? can we make the boundary more explicit instead?`
- `this looks like a bespoke helper for something we already have elsewhere. can we reuse the canonical one?`
- `i think there's a code-judo move here that makes this much simpler. can we reframe this so these branches disappear?`
- `this refactor moves complexity around, but doesn't really delete it. is there a way to make the model itself simpler?`

## Output Expectations

Prioritize findings in this order:

1. Structural code-quality regressions
2. Missed opportunities for dramatic simplification / code-judo restructuring
3. Spaghetti / branching complexity increases
4. Boundary / abstraction / type-contract problems that make the code harder to reason about
5. File-size and decomposition concerns
6. Modularity and abstraction issues
7. Legibility and maintainability concerns

Do not flood the review with low-value nits if there are larger structural issues.
Prefer a smaller number of high-conviction comments over a long list of cosmetic notes.

## Approval Bar

Do not approve merely because behavior seems correct.
The bar for approval is:

- no clear structural regression
- no obvious missed opportunity to make the implementation dramatically simpler when such a path is visible
- no unjustified file-size explosion
- no obvious spaghetti-growth from special-case branching
- no obviously hacky or magical abstraction that makes the code harder to reason about
- no unnecessary wrapper/cast/optionality churn obscuring the real design
- no clear architecture-boundary leak or avoidable canonical-helper duplication
- no missed opportunity for an obvious decomposition that would materially improve maintainability

Treat these as presumptive blockers unless the author can justify them clearly:

- the PR preserves a lot of incidental complexity when there is a plausible code-judo move that would delete it
- the PR pushes a file from below 1000 lines to above 1000 lines
- the PR adds ad-hoc branching that makes an existing flow more tangled
- the PR solves a local problem by scattering feature checks across shared code
- the PR adds an unnecessary abstraction, wrapper, or cast-heavy contract that makes the design more indirect
- the PR duplicates an existing helper or puts logic in the wrong layer when there is a clear canonical home

If those conditions are not met, leave explicit, actionable feedback and push for a cleaner decomposition.

<!-- END_PROJECT_Z_PAYLOAD -->

---
name: test-audit
description: "Invoke whenever writing, changing, reviewing, or sweeping tests. Authoring gate for new tests plus audit workflow for low-value, implementation-coupled, or duplicative tests and the test-only production seams they demand."
---

# Test Audit

Three modes, one value bar. Authoring mode gates every new or changed test at
write time. Audit mode runs focused sweeps of tests that re-assert source,
duplicate stronger proof, couple behavior to implementation, or keep test-only
production seams alive. Continue broad audits as separate coherent follow-up
PRs; optimize for confidence, not deletion count. Campaign mode prunes one
whole subsystem's test surface (every test file a plugin or core area owns);
before starting one, read [CAMPAIGN.md](CAMPAIGN.md).

## Authoring gate

Before adding any test, answer four questions; a missing answer means do not
add it yet:

1. What observable behavior, invariant, or independent contract does it protect?
2. What credible regression makes it fail?
3. Why does existing coverage not already catch that failure? Each contract has
   one primary test owner at the strongest boundary; another layer needs its
   own distinct risk, such as a transport or lifecycle failure the owner cannot
   reach. Prefer extending a table-driven case or shared fixture over a
   near-duplicate test; consolidate duplicated setup in the same change.
4. Does it need a production seam (export, flag, wrapper, injection hook) that no
   production caller needs? If yes, move the test to the real boundary instead.

For production-path and failure proof, use the [boundary guide](../../../docs/help/testing/writing-tests.md#prove-behavior-at-the-owning-boundary); integration tests face the same value bar below.

Then check the test against every [junk pattern](#junk-patterns); a match fails
the gate unless the [retention bar](#retention-bar) names the contract it
independently guards. A test that would break under behavior-preserving
refactoring is asserting implementation, not behavior; rewrite it at the
owning boundary before landing it.

Bug regression tests must fail on the pre-fix code for the intended reason and
pass after the owner-boundary repair. A regression test that never demonstrably
failed proves the mock, not the fix. One regression at the owner boundary
covers the bug; do not replay the same scenario at every layer it crosses.

## Junk patterns

The shared checklist for both modes: the authoring gate rejects a new test that
matches one, and audits hunt for existing tests that do.

- assertion-free coverage probes;
- self-comparisons and identity copiers;
- copied fixtures, inventories, manifests, or export lists;
- exact source, import, or string greps;
- private predicate or call-shape tests duplicated at real boundaries;
- duplicate invocations of the same contract;
- provider-local replays of shared helpers;
- tests whose only purpose is preserving test-only exports, globals, or wrappers;
- dead production code whose only callers are tests;
- expected values produced by the helper or renderer under test;
- mocks that implement the asserted behavior, or one identical mock standing in
  for different APIs;
- fixtures that supply the receipt, admission, or callback ordering the owner
  should produce, or persistence asserted against a store the path never writes;
- capability tests that restate declared flags instead of exercising the
  delivery or acknowledgement the flag promises;
- negative controls that pass for an unrelated reason, such as a denial from a
  different guard or a rejection the production path never reaches;
- names or fixtures that promise more than the input exercises, such as a
  "retires the window" test asserting the window was not cleared.

## Value bar

Tests justify their maintenance cost by protecting behavior, a credible
regression, or an independently meaningful contract. In an audit, an existing
test that must change for behavior-preserving source reorganization is suspect,
not automatically deletable; the authoring gate still rejects new ones.

Before judging a candidate, read the complete test and production owner, its
entry point, callers, callees, sibling implementations, overlapping tests, CI
routing, and relevant history. Read root and scoped `AGENTS.md` files first.
When the test claims dependency-backed behavior, inspect the dependency source
or types directly.

## Discovery

Keep discovery read-only and report evidence before editing. For broad scope,
run parallel discovery lanes when available:

- core and packages (`src/`, `packages/`);
- plugins (`extensions/`);
- UI, apps, scripts, and tooling;
- a cross-cutting pattern sweep.

Outside campaign mode, prefer a few high-confidence candidates over a large
speculative inventory. Hunt for the [junk patterns](#junk-patterns).

## Retention bar

Keep a test when it independently enforces a public API, plugin SDK, protocol,
config, migration, storage, security, platform, default, prompt-byte, generated
cross-language, package, release, or architecture contract. Also keep:

- call ordering when order is observable behavior;
- regressions with a credible failure mode;
- source inspection when it is the cheapest independent guard: it fails when
  the contract changes (the user-facing key, byte, or path) and survives an
  identifier-only refactor;
- a retained test that fails on the baseline: treat it as a possible product
  bug, reproduce it, and repair the owner rather than deleting it.

Static or slow is not a deletion reason. A test that resembles implementation
may still be the independent contract; prove otherwise before removing it.

## Candidate evidence

Record every field below before editing. A missing field means the candidate is
not ready for deletion:

- exact test name and location;
- what failure it can actually detect;
- non-test callers of the covered production or support seam;
- stronger remaining owner-boundary proof, or why no proof is needed;
- relevant history and the reason the test or seam exists;
- production or test-support deletion unlocked;
- risk and the focused validation command.

## Edit shape

Choose one coherent owner-boundary batch. Delete obsolete test-only exports,
globals, wrappers, and dead production paths instead of preserving aliases.
Move retained regressions to their canonical owners. Consolidate repeated
package or dependency assertions into one generic contract.

Prefer net-negative production LOC. Do not add replacement tests that restate
the same implementation, and do not convert uncertain candidates into cleanup
to increase deletion counts.

## Validation

Never edit source or tests while Vitest is running in the checkout. Follow
`$openclaw-testing`; route heavy proof through its `$crabbox` rules.

1. Run the smallest owner and sibling tests with
   `node scripts/run-vitest.mjs <path-or-filter>`.
2. For removed source greps or plan assertions, run the executable script or
   dry-run that owns the real contract.
3. Run targeted formatting, then `git diff --check`.
4. Classify with
   `node scripts/check-changed.mjs --dry-run -- <changed-paths>`, then run the
   actual changed gate required by repository policy.
5. Inspect `git diff --numstat`; report production/tooling separately from
   tests and test support.
6. After final audit edits, run mandatory `$autoreview`.

## Landing and continuation

Commit, push, open a PR, or land only when authorized. Use
`$openclaw-pr-maintainer` and the repository `scripts/pr` flow. Land one
coherent PR at a time; after landing, refresh from current `main` and rerun
read-only discovery for the next high-confidence batch.

## Handoff

Report:

- root cause and removed low-value categories;
- production owner simplifications;
- retained false positives and why they remain valuable;
- focused and full proof actually run;
- production versus test LOC;
- PR and merge state;
- named follow-ups.

name: project-z-verification
description: Verify a Project Z phase through an isolated real native owner flow, independent original-semantics checks, mounted Work, physical recovery and a separately installed rebuild. Use for Z0-Z5 implementation or handoff verification, not as evidence that this source-grounded procedure has already run.

# Project Z verification

## Authority and honest current state

The full locked Project Z PRD and common contracts govern. All LLM work uses
gpt-6.1-sol/max, no fallback. This skill does not authorize provisioning,
subagents, external sends, production access, grants, signers, paid effects,
commits, pushes or deployments. Current phase authority and coordinator
ownership decide writes. Protected admission belongs to independent custody.

This is a **new source-grounded skill**, not the unavailable historical
boundary-verification skill. At authoring, product-runtime verification and
separately installed reconstruction are NOT_RUN. Do not claim this skill
already drove a real Work, authenticated a user or verified a Z mechanism.

## Doctor: establish an owned target before mutations

1. Read AGENTS.md, the complete seed, its lock/receipt and mandatory companion
   read map. Run `python3 scope-z/prompts/assemble.py check` from the actual
   destination repository. Check all original lock members, source commit/tree,
   delivery inventory and active own phase. Missing bytes are NOT_DELIVERED.
2. Record actual `git rev-parse HEAD`, `git rev-parse HEAD^{tree}`,
   `git status --short`, Node/npm/Python/native compiler versions, package/
   dependency lock hashes and installed build identity. Run commands with
   an explicit absolute repository cwd. Root requires Node 22.x. A source
   hash or requested model flag is not an installed-runtime receipt.
3. Before product code, write feature/failure/expected-value maps. Read real
   callers and installed Next documentation for relevant edits. Inventory
   environment **names only**, never print credentials or .env contents.
   Require an explicitly authorized disposable tenant/database, real local
   test-owner credentials, same frontend/API Supabase Auth project and owned
   process/port identities. Disable live providers/effects; loopback alone
   does not make inherited auth/provider connections disposable.
4. Source-grounded launcher:
   `node scripts/centropy/start-certification-stack.mjs --name=project-z-proof`.
   It writes .centropy-certification/project-z-proof/stack.json, migrates and
   seeds a persistent disposable DB and starts frontend/API/worker. Its ports
   are fixed 3001/3101/55441 even for different names. Run one such stack at a
   time; do not borrow or kill another person's listener. It deletes
   AUTH_DEV_BYPASS and requires configured real Auth. If auth or isolation is
   unavailable, record the blocker; a fake owner or bypass is not a substitute.
   `--production-build` exists but requires both actual .next/BUILD_ID files.
5. Root check commands actually present: `npm run workspace:check`,
   `npm run centropy:manifest:check`, `npm run centropy:coverage:check`,
   `npm run centropy:client:check`, `npm run centropy:forms:check`.
   Workspace typecheck: `npm --prefix finnor-os run typecheck`.
   Root `npm run lint` and `npm run build` exist. Run only affected checks
   with bounded resources and an actual exit/status receipt. The release
   generated-preflight chain includes writers, so do not call it a doctor.

Do not run a launcher, migration, seed or fault injection in a prompt-only
task. Do not print URLs containing credentials or export them into evidence.

## Drive: actual actions and durable identity

Existing basic browser check, after the owned stack is ready:

```sh
cd /absolute/path/to/the/owned/repository &&
PLAYWRIGHT_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:3001 \
PLAYWRIGHT_OUTPUT_DIR=test-results/project-z-scratch \
node scripts/centropy/run-local-e2e.mjs \
e2e/centropy-owner-login.spec.ts --project=desktop-chromium --workers=1
```

The path above is documented syntax, not an active unresolved phase field.
Select the actual absolute checkout before executing it. The launcher loads
test-owner credentials locally and enforces loopback. It does not provision
the stack. The test checks /api/centropy/me and reload, not Project Z semantics.
All skipped tests mean NOT_RUN for the skipped behavior, never PASSED.

For phase proof, use the existing mounted /centropy login, root/Work controls
and real authenticated actions/query interfaces. Existing selectors include
Email, Password, Sign in, Message CENTROPY, Send instruction, and the CENTROPY
conversation region. Recheck actual installed selectors instead of assuming
the snapshot is current. Record sanitized inputs and actual returned original
Work/thread/input IDs. Do not invent a new /api/project-z endpoint or claim
a phase-specific runner exists before implementing it.

Trace the same original ID through actual job claim/fence, bounded producer,
independent checker process, persisted artifact and native owner consumer.
Bind current rights/source/terms/code/checker/consumer and parent
reservation/deadline/budget. Exercise shared S5 and S6 only within explicit
disposable authority. Preserve independently observed effects or UNKNOWN.
Include negative tenant/private-use, stale/revoked, unsupported, incomplete
and resource cases, plus a real useful supported positive.

|---|---|
| Z0 | Real authenticated HTTP/DB query identity, canonical inventory denominator, original-parent reachability |
| Z1 | Original per-Work direct exact initial/update/edit/retraction computation, not the shared DAG |

## Observe: actions plus persisted and user-visible state

For every feature, retain action, expected-value provenance, authenticated
response, durable original-ID evidence and reloaded mounted Work. Screenshots
alone do not prove DB state; SQL alone does not prove owner consumption.
Observe computation disposition, source/currentness, checker result, owner
use, S5 capacity, S6 obligations/UNKNOWN/reconciliation, S7 costs, exact
conditionality, security limits and separate qualification states.

Use actual Work reads such as /api/centropy/works/{actualWorkId} under the
authenticated proxy; braces here document URL syntax, not a fabricated ID.
An original counterexample, rejected falsifier or unsupported result remains
visible. Completion means the independent expected state, not disappearance
of an error. Reload, switch session/root and use keyboard/semantic controls
where relevant. Cover all mapped user-visible features, not just login.

## Physical recovery and remaining-budget fallback

Kill only an owned producer/worker at declared durable boundaries, then
restart the actual worker from the same owned stack/source build. Prove claim
fencing, duplicate handling, preserved original IDs, reservations, deadlines,
remaining resource receipts and no unauthorized effect replay. Record a real
physical exit and restart, not only an in-memory exception. Retain UNKNOWN
and outstanding S6 obligations through timeout/cancel/restart.

Existing e2e/centropy-controlled-recovery.spec.ts is a narrower example. It
requires CENTROPY_DISPOSABLE_E2E=1 and a guarded disposable admin DB at
127.0.0.1:55441/finnor, temporarily changes a read permission and restores it
in finally. It is a privileged mutation, not a harmless smoke test, and it
does not prove once-only external effects. Use it only with explicit local
DB authority; never infer that authority from this skill.

## Separate installed proof and replay artifact

Rebuild/install the phase's final source on a separately isolated target with
no borrowed node_modules, undeclared local Git objects, hidden native assets,
source fallback or unrecorded dependency/toolchain. Record exact source/base/
head/tree, install/build commands, dependency and native asset hashes,
build IDs, runtime target, authenticated original Work IDs and repeated
owner consumption/UI proof. Source E2E and installed E2E have separate states.
No frozen final implementation means installed-development is not PASSED.

Each finite run retains an immutable artifact outside Playwright's output
directory, which Playwright clears on a later run. Include sanitized inputs,
steps, independent expected values, actual observations, exit codes, skips,
source/build/toolchain, seed/pack/lock digests, evidence hashes, replay command,
environment variable names, complete costs/meter gaps, failed attempts,
remaining budget/obligations and coverage/gate dispositions.

Existing browser proofs include actorEmail. Restrict originals locally and
make redacted derivatives before any explicitly authorized delivery; never
send tokens, credentialed URLs, PII, private observations or live data.
Keep failure proofs and their digests when rerunning. A later pass does not
erase a previous failure or justify changing its expected value.

## Cleanup and final coverage

Stop only owned recorded stack/process IDs, preferably signal the launcher so
it stops its children/embedded database. Do not broad-match processes or
delete another fixture. Restore explicitly injected local permissions, retain
the persistent disposable database/evidence as required for replay, close
owned browser contexts and record remaining reservations/UNKNOWN. Do not
delete exposure or call cancellation compensation.

Report each feature's doctor/drive/observe/recovery/installed coverage.
Separate doc drift from product defects and separate internal incompleteness
from real external custody/authority blockers. Apply the current common
qualification states to each gate. Final phase handoff uses IMPLEMENTED,
CHANGED, EVIDENCE, COMPLETION GATE, REMAINING LIMITATIONS, FRONTIER DELTA,
NEXT DEPENDENCIES. No assembly check, login smoke, checker microbenchmark or
this skill's existence establishes protected admission, operational/financial
100×, whole-code correspondence or production activation.

capacity. An unavailable provider or unqualified model is not success.

current capability, original parent, job/checker/consumer, observation or
UNKNOWN, costs and separate gate states. A generated dashboard is not repair.

affected X/Y regressions. Missing integrated source or mount is internal
incompleteness, not an external custody blocker.

Report no Z0 novelty and no 100× result from repair timing. Full later
qualification still requires ≥30 independent institution-period blocks,
material accepted-work load curves, all five increments and separate S7 gates.

/goal
Complete Z1's SharedWorkProgram: construct and exactly maintain shared pure computation across distinct lawful material Works, while keeping each original result, rights, resources and native effect separate. Prove original per-Work refinement, actual owner use and full-lifecycle equipped value rather than identical-query caching.

## OUTCOME AND COMPLETION PREDICATE

Use full PRD §5. SharedWorkProgram contains the common envelope; original
Work/programme/input/output semantics; typed shared computation and per-Work
projection; lawful partition/dependency graph; initial/delta/edit/retraction
rules; exact refinement/coverage certificate; per-Work currentness/accounting;
checkpoint recovery/invalidation. It is a proposed increment, not established.

For original pure programmes require
$\pi_i(P(D,\Theta))=f_i(D,\theta_i)$ for every original Work. For signed
insertions/retractions $\delta$ and programme edits $\eta$ require
$\pi_i(U_P(P(D,\Theta),\delta,\eta))=
f_i^\eta(D\oplus\delta,\theta_i^\eta)$.
The independent checker derives each original $f_i$, not just the shared DAG.
No shared business/effect identity, changed utility or widened right.

Initial support: 2–1000 Works, ≤4096 DAG nodes, ≤100000 source rows,
≤1000000 derived rows, ≤512-bit rational components. Exact typed finite pure
integer/rational DAGs support declared filters/projections/joins/unions,
grouped sums/comparisons and deterministic bounded selections with original
units, currencies, rounding, bag/set multiplicities and ties. Floating
semantics, unbounded recursion and effects are unsupported. Size/overflow/
deadline breach is INCOMPLETE/UNSUPPORTED or an eligible direct fallback
under remaining resources, never silent approximation.

## VERIFIED STARTING POINT AND INTEGRATION BOUNDARIES

## IMPLEMENTATION FREEDOM AND REQUIRED ENGINEERING METHODS

Read every full pinned skill and the new verification skill. Write domain and
failure models before code. In this session compare whole-shape indexed
shared DAG/delta maintenance with independent per-Work evaluation plus
ordinary caches/indexes; choose the smallest complete exact design justified
by actual overlap and lifetime. Hash-consing, algebraic rewrites, DBSP/FIVM,
differential views and self-adjusting methods are equipment, not novelty.

Discover sharing across **nonidentical** dependency graphs. Partition by
tenant/principal/permitted use/revision/security class; no cross-tenant raw
data or private-feature reuse. When sharing is not exact or worthwhile,
recompute directly and charge it. Programme edits include objectives,
thresholds, source revisions and withdrawn Works. Withdrawal releases only
reversible computation, never historical provenance or outstanding S6
exposure. Allocate shared costs reproducibly, with totals conserved.

Implement producer, independent original-semantics checker, durable candidate
job, per-Work native consumers, currentness, meters and packaging together.
No new workflow/truth system. Read actual callers and installed Next guides
before relevant edits. No post-code unit tests. Use real finite failure-first
E2E and narrow type/lint/build checks; preserve failures rather than weaken
expectations.

## ACCEPTANCE EVIDENCE

**A. Mechanism.** Recompute every original Work independently on initial,
delta, edit and retraction inputs. Check all admitted rewrite/delta rules,
coverage, output units, empty groups, join multiplicities, ties, negative
retractions and arithmetic bounds, not only a producer-selected shared graph.

**B. Adversarial semantics.** Different covenant/liquidity/approval/readiness
Works share lawful source transforms. Change one source affecting only some
Works, edit an objective/threshold, retract a row, revoke a source and withdraw
a Work while another retains an obligation. A stale apparently unaffected
view, duplicated row, lost negative multiplicity or merged effect ID fails.

**C. Uncertainty.** Preserve original evidence/model alternatives and UNKNOWN.
Do not collapse unknown source quality, forecast or price into exact fact.
Out-of-profile or exhausted computation stays unsupported/incomplete, not an
approximately checked exact result.

**D. Information.** Exercise tenant, principal, private field, permitted-use,
security-class and revocation partitions including cache/intermediate metadata
leaks. Sharing must not grant another Work data it cannot lawfully use.

**E. Currentness.** Bind and mutate source, rights, programme objective,
parameter, unit and checker/code revisions before consumption. Recompute/
invalidate exact dependencies and preserve per-Work histories. Withdrawn Work
history and liabilities remain attributable.

**F. Authenticated durable positive owner flow.** Actual original Works request
a registered candidate job, durable claim, independent checker and persisted
SharedWorkProgram. Real S3/S4 consume separate projections, S5 checks shared
capacity, S6 sees only original separate authorized actions; retain independent
observation or UNKNOWN, S7 charges and the same mounted Works.

**G. Physical interruption/recovery.** Terminate the real worker after claim,
checkpoint/artifact persistence and delivery before owner acknowledgement.
Restart with fencing and duplicate delivery; compare all original results,
outstanding obligations and conserved charges. Eligible direct fallback uses
the same remaining parent budget/deadline, not renewed work.

**H. Mounted Work.** Reload and switch among original Works. Show own result,
shared dependency/currentness, checker and actual consumer, reversible
withdrawal state, resources, costs, remaining obligations and gate states.
No new shared-effect badge or unauthenticated substitute surface.

**I. Final separately installed reconstruction.** Pin final integrated source,
dependencies, native assets and Node 22.x. Rebuild separately without borrowed
modules/source fallback and repeat initial/update/edit/retraction/recovery/
revocation and real per-Work owner/mount proof. Record source and installed
dispositions separately.

**J. Equipped comparison/ablation.** Compare direct full qualified X/Y and
tuned caches/indexes/DBSP/FIVM-compatible methods plus an equipped joint
compiler. Include low-overlap independent graphs, real actual reuse horizon
and all discovery/compile/storage/warmup/update/check/failure/invalidation/
reconstruction costs. At least five alternating diagnostic repetitions;
identical-query-only wins, fewer real assignments or hidden uncharged parents
fail. Respect original observation/approval/effect bottlenecks.

Freeze the public 32-family registration and ≥8 fresh cases per family before
implementation. Require ≥95% independently accepted useful supported positives,
all critical soundness/authority/information falsifiers rejected and all
claimed infeasibility checked; report per-family failures. Public cases do
not administer the protected oracle or replace ≥30 institution-period blocks.
Use one finite registered sweep plus affected bounded reruns and the three
declared physical interruption points, with immutable replay artifacts.

## EXECUTION CONTROL, FINISH DISCIPLINE AND HANDOFF

Execute only under actual Computer/delivery/model/phase authorization. Every
LLM role is gpt-6.1-sol/max, no fallback, no unapproved subagents/missions.
Read the full common execution and CLI annexes. Do not provision, push, PR,
merge, deploy, grant/sign, contact outsiders or create real financial effects.
Local commits require actual phase authority. Keep global files coordinator-
owned and preserve concurrent user work. No session-id with spec-model/use-spec.

Separate architecture, internal-software, installed-development-reconstruction,
mathematical-security-domain, protected-admission, equipped-increment,
operational-100x, financial-headroom, financial-100x, production-activation.
Use truthful common states. No exact checking implies native authority,
protected admission, capacity 100× or financial 100×. Missing internal
producer/checker/adapter/mount/meters/install is incomplete, not external.

Submit the lightweight immutable scheduling handoff promptly, independently
of deep certification: exact source/base/head/tree and changed files,
product/pack/seed/build/interfaces, actual receipts, finite verification and
retained failures, separate install/replay commands, shared patches,
complete costs/gaps, remaining budgets/deadlines/obligations and actual peers.
Finish all achievable engineering; preserve genuine independent custody/
comparison/field blockers for their gates only.

End with exactly **IMPLEMENTED** / **CHANGED** / **EVIDENCE** /
**COMPLETION GATE** / **REMAINING LIMITATIONS** / **FRONTIER DELTA** /
**NEXT DEPENDENCIES**. A useful implementation with no equipped increment
is an honest failed increment, not a renamed inherited capability.

/goal
Complete Z2's CoalitionResilientProtocol: construct useful native dynamic protocols that retain the full Y3 unilateral sequential/common-belief floor and exclude the declared ex ante weak-Pareto-profitable correlated joint contingent deviations. Independently reconstruct original lawful plans, integrate real participant-local owner flows and report impossible families honestly.

## OUTCOME AND COMPLETION PREDICATE

Use full PRD §6 and the complete supplied Y2/Y3 blueprints. The principal
CoalitionResilientProtocol binds the common envelope, original admitted models,
economic/operator and local information meanings, candidate protocol, full
Y3 evidence, coalition/timing/threat profile, complete lawful joint-plan
coverage, exact counterstrategy or dual exclusion witnesses, fixed terms/
consent, participant-local execution/currentness and cost/recovery contract.
This is a proposed increment, not a proved scientific result.

For every admitted model $m$, nonempty coalition $C$ up to $k=n$, and original
lawful joint contingent plan $d$, derive
$g_{i,m}(d)=E_m[u_i(d,\sigma_{-C})]-E_m[u_i(\sigma)]$.
Exclude every $p\ge0$, $\mathbf1^Tp=1$, $G_mp\ge0$ with
$\mathbf1^TG_mp>0$. The exact rational LP maximizes that last expression.
A checked no-profit dual has $\lambda_i\ge0$, $\nu\le0$ and, for **every**
original $d$, $\nu\ge\sum_{i\in C}(1+\lambda_i)g_{i,m}(d)$.
The summed normalized gain is a feasibility device, not cash or owner value.

Coalitions commit ex ante before the specified private draw; plans may use
all later lawful local histories/messages. This is not interim coalition-proof
sequential equilibrium, arbitrary later coalition formation, MASE or Abraham
k-resilience. Preserve full Y3 sequential rationality and beliefs from **one
common global tremble sequence**, participation/nonanticipativity/disclosure,
original utility scales/outside options/legal transfers/budgets and consents.

Profile: 2–4 participants, ≥3 dates in dynamic positives, at least two private
state values where supplied, ≤64 original information sets, ≤128 candidate
protocol policies, ≤4096 lawful pure joint plans per coalition, ≤512-bit
rationals. Exceeding bounds cannot prune the original family to manufacture
stability. CHECKED_INFEASIBLE requires checked complete candidate-family
coverage, not a failed search or a rejected candidate.

## IMPLEMENTATION FREEDOM AND REQUIRED ENGINEERING METHODS

Apply all twelve pinned skills and the verification skill. Before code record
failure/domain maps and compare whole-shape complete enumeration plus exact LP
with counterstrategy-guided candidate refinement retaining the same complete
independent checker. Choose the smallest complete bounded construction.
Approximate LP tolerances, sampled plans or heuristic best responses are not
absence certificates. Ordinary game/LP libraries are equipment.

Compile original chance support, local information sets, legal communication,
transfers and operator meanings into complete lawful joint contingent plans.
Do not grant impossible pooled private state or delete a lawful side-payment/
communication plan. Construct/select a protocol using already authorized
evidence/commitment rules, not human redesign, invented penalties, legal powers
or changed utilities. Reject robustness if any admitted model has a profitable
mixture; a narrower profile is a separately labelled result.

Run native full Y3 checks plus independently reconstructed exact joint checks
before real owner use. Implement durable bounded producer/checker, persistence,

## ACCEPTANCE EVIDENCE

**A. Mechanism.** Independently reconstruct every lawful original joint plan
for every admitted model and nonempty coalition up to k=n; verify exact
primal profitable mixture or all dual inequalities, candidate coverage,
and the full native Y3 sequential/common-tremble floor. No convenient off-path
belief or per-information-set unrelated tremble sequence.

**B. Adversarial semantics.** A dynamic protocol passes unilateral checks yet
fails coordinated early-report/later-action deviation. The compiler constructs
or selects an already lawful alternative. Also check gain vectors (4,-2) and
(-1,1): mixture 1/4 and 3/4 gives (1/4,1/4), so pure-only/coarse-grid checks
must fail. Include useful exact stable dynamic and genuinely impossible full
families. Payoff-vector arithmetic alone is not the business positive.

**C. Uncertainty.** Evaluate every admitted model separately, including off-path
and rare chance support; no averaging away a profitable model. Retain
model-relative/partial/UNKNOWN versus observed compliance. Incomplete
enumeration never becomes checked infeasibility.

**D. Information.** Test private pooling, anticipatory plans, omitted lawful
messages/side payments and unauthorized disclosures. Participants see only
lawful local histories; correlated coordination does not imply omniscience.
Check tenant/principal/log/proof leaks too.

**E. Currentness.** Mutate model/operator/right/source revisions, legal transfer
rules, terms and consents before dispatch and after restart. Invalidate
the protocol and recheck under the original parent; no changed terms reuse
of old consent or renewed budget/deadline.

**F. Authenticated durable positive owner flow.** Original Work causes a real
registered claim/producer/independent checker/persisted protocol. Native Y3/
S4 creates participant-local Works, S5 retains aggregate feasibility, S6
retains original terms/consents/obligations; independent observation or UNKNOWN,
S7 full costs and mounted original Work are real, not mocked consumers.

**G. Physical interruption/recovery.** Kill at durable protocol claim,
persisted checked artifact and partially instantiated participant Work/consent
boundary. Restart/redeliver with fencing, original IDs and no duplicate effect.
Fallback uses only remaining original budget/deadline and fixed legal terms;
UNKNOWN and unresolved S6 liabilities survive.

**H. Mounted Work.** Reload participant and parent views. Show exact ex ante
incentive concept, excluded threat classes, original model/terms/checker,
counterstrategy or dual, local disclosure/consent, current owner state,
resources, UNKNOWN/costs and separate gates. Never a generic “safe” badge.

**I. Final separately installed reconstruction.** Rebuild final integrated
source/dependencies/native solver assets separately, Node 22.x, no borrowed
modules, hidden solver or source fallback. Repeat dynamic construction,
mixture falsifier, full Y3 floor, owner/consent/recovery and mounted proof.
Record exact rational toolchain and actual exit/skips.

**J. Equipped comparison/ablation.** Compare full qualified X/Y plus tuned
ordinary game/LP methods and equipped coalition construction at complete
lifecycle cost, candidate rejects/checks/model enumeration/consents/recovery
included. Use ≥5 alternating diagnostics without confusing them with protected
power. MASE/rational secret sharing libraries are equipment, not inherited
FINNOR guarantees. Any lawful profitable correlated continuation or weakened
unilateral floor falsifies the claim.

Freeze ≥32 independently specified structural families and ≥8 fresh cases

/goal
Complete Z3's ViabilityStrategy: synthesize and independently check finite public-state strategies that preserve original safety on every play and achieve precisely the declared conditional recurring goals, with explicit resource/UNKNOWN meanings and real native continuation use. Never substitute a safe wait loop or fairness-vacuous controller for progress.

## OUTCOME AND COMPLETION PREDICATE

Use full PRD §7. ViabilityStrategy binds common envelope; original mandate/
observation/arena; initial/safety/fairness/recurrence semantics; complete
winning region and fixed-point evidence; finite memory/action/update maps;
independent all-play progress and nonvacuity checks; resource renewal/UNKNOWN/
S6 interpretation; original behavior refinement and revision bridge.

For a finite total turn-based public perfect-information arena
$G=(V_C\uplus V_E,E,I,S,\{A_i\},\{B_j\})$, require every original initial
state and every permitted environment play under the extracted strategy:
$\mathbf G S\land[(\bigwedge_i\mathbf{GF}A_i)\Rightarrow
(\bigwedge_j\mathbf{GF}B_j)]$.
Safety is outside the fairness implication. Explicit deadlock/terminal
semantics must not create vacuous wins. No fairness assumptions means the
unconditional assumption set V, not an empty implication.

Use existential controller/universal environment CPre. Compute the safety
kernel $\nu Z.(S\cap CPre(Z))$, then correctly qualified GR(1)/generalized-
Büchi iteration and actual memory extraction. A fixed point alone is not
proof of the complete winning region. A rejecting strategy is not unrealizability.

Profile: ≤256 explicit states, ≤8 outgoing choices per state, ≤2 environment
justice sets, ≤2 recurring goals and explicit bounded strategy memory.
Only genuinely public fully observed arenas; uncertain outcomes are original
environment moves. No hidden-state oracle, invented fairness/renewal,
non-Zeno/deadline or per-request completion inference from GF. Actual
nonrenewing resources, permanent UNKNOWN and authority denials remain.

## VERIFIED STARTING POINT AND INTEGRATION BOUNDARIES

## IMPLEMENTATION FREEDOM AND REQUIRED ENGINEERING METHODS

Apply all full pinned skills and verification procedure. Before code define
original state/resource/observation semantics and failure model. Compare a
direct explicit-state qualified game solver with symbolic/abstraction-assisted
synthesis plus an explicit original-arena checker; choose the smallest complete
bounded design. Do not hide lost transitions in abstraction or drop universal
environment choices for speed. Standard GR(1) is not automatically a proof of
nonconflicting synthesis or its completeness.

The independent checker reconstructs originals, verifies every least/greatest
iterant or equivalent complete certificate, legality/totality/memory
initialization/update, and the entire reachable arena×strategy-memory graph.
For each B_j remove B_j and examine **all cyclic SCCs**, including non-bottom
ones, intersecting every A_i. Check assumption satisfiability and controller-
induced vacuity/non-conflict separately. UNKNOWN is required if the stronger
nonconflicting/completeness claim is unresolved.

Implement bounded producer/checker, durable registered job, actual S4
continuation adapter, persistence/currentness, resource/obligation-preserving
revision bridge, meters and installed packaging together. Read actual callers
and installed Next guides before relevant edits. No post-code unit tests or

## ACCEPTANCE EVIDENCE

**A. Mechanism.** Independently verify original arena coverage and initial
states, complete region evidence and the reachable strategy-memory graph.
Cross-check all arenas in the registered ≤8-state reference population with
an independently implemented exhaustive solver; certify its finite population
definition instead of pretending all labelled eight-state arenas were run.
Reject all unsafe reachable states and fair bad cyclic SCCs.

**B. Adversarial semantics.** Include safe wait-only/no-progress loop,
attractive unserviceable cycle, non-bottom fair bad SCC, controller-induced
fairness failure, nonrenewing budget exhaustion and a genuinely winning
recurring controlled readiness/recovery case. Both source-edge/goal/policy
mutations and strategy-memory mistakes must fail.

**C. Uncertainty.** Permanent UNKNOWN can lock capacity forever; model it and
retain conditionality. An environment can deny progress within its permitted
choices. Report ordinary winning, assumption-satisfiable, nonconflicting and
unrealizable/unknown distinctions. GF does not guarantee each request finishes
or bound physical elapsed time.

**D. Information.** Two indistinguishable observations requiring opposite
actions cannot pass the public perfect-information profile by granting
private state. Test tenant/principal/source-use leakage and lawful public
observation refinements; unsupported partial observation remains unsupported.

**E. Currentness.** Mutate original transitions, safety/justice/goals,
observation meanings, resource renewal, rights and code/checker revision.
Invalidate/rederive under the same parent. A changed arena or convenient
fairness assumption cannot reuse the old certificate.

**F. Authenticated durable positive owner flow.** Original Work enters actual
queue/claim, synthesis, independent check and persistence. S4 consumes the
real strategy and memory, S5 checks current resources, S6 retains effects/
UNKNOWN/obligations, independent observation and S7 costs return to the same
mounted Work. A solver-only win is insufficient.

**G. Physical interruption/recovery.** Terminate the owned worker after
durable claim, strategy/memory checkpoint and uncertain native observation.
Restart/redeliver and prove fencing, memory/refinement, same reservation/
deadline/obligations and remaining-budget fallback. No restart-created
renewal or erased UNKNOWN.

**H. Mounted Work.** Reload and switch context; show original state,
strategy/memory/region, exact safety and conditional progress, fairness/
nonvacuity status, next authorized action, UNKNOWN/resources/costs and separate
gates. Never label a possible completion path inevitable completion.

**I. Final separately installed reconstruction.** Rebuild final integrated
source, solver/toolchain/native assets/dependencies on a separate clean
Node 22.x target; no hidden solvers or source fallback. Repeat useful recurring
owner behavior, reference/negative SCC cases, physical restart and mounted
conditionality. Source and installed states are separate.

**J. Equipped comparison/ablation.** Compare full qualified X/Y equipped with
standard synthesis/model checking, tuned explicit/symbolic methods and
ordinary continuations. Cost arena extraction, complete exploration, strategy/
checker memory, invalidation/recovery and actual use at registered demand.
Use ≥5 alternating diagnostics; finite prefix success or removed environment
moves cannot create an increment or operational 100×.

Freeze ≥32 independent structural families with ≥8 fresh cases each from the

Demonstrate finite-range verifier/proof scaling and full-lifecycle demand crossover, not a trace, signature or verifier-only victory.

## OUTCOME AND COMPLETION PREDICATE

Use full PRD §8. ExecutionProofCapsule binds common envelope; actual function/
programme and step relation; initial/final states and input/output commitments;
types/units/rounding/range/overflow meanings; original interpreter/compiler
correspondence; actual proof, verification parameters and assumptions; length/
code/version/security binding; checker/cost/currentness and owner contract.

For actual runtime-defined F and steps require the exact relation
$R_F(s_t,x_t,s_{t+1})$, composed for the claimed original length. Bind all
code/constants/types/units/rounding/commitments/states/length/version. A valid
field proof for the wrong integer semantics fails. Trace replay, hash chains,
signatures, Y4 confidentiality/MPC or a Y5 theorem wrapper are not this output.
